# Coordination: recovery plan (two agents + owner)

Plan: https://claude.ai/code/artifact/83f2cea6-8e1b-4a46-9614-e12d2186ba24 · Context: `RECOVERY_HANDOFF.md`
Branch: `experiment/recovery-plan`

- **LOCAL** = Claude Code in the owner's terminal. It has the shell, Docker and git.
- **CLOUD** = the claude.ai session. It reads and writes files in this folder and computes in its own workspace, but runs nothing on this PC.
- **OWNER** = Ali.

## Rules

1. Before starting a task, read this file and set its status to `in progress (<agent>, <date time>)`. When it's
   done, set `done` with one line: the result and the files changed. Then append a line to the Log.
2. **File ownership.** Neither agent edits the other's files; ask in the Log instead.
   - LOCAL owns: `services/**` (except `services/model-api/analysis/phase*`), `apps/`, `sdk/`, `extension/`,
     `packages/`, `scripts/`, the compose files, `RUN_LOG.md`, `TODO.md`, `HANDOFF.md`, the manuscript and pages.
   - CLOUD owns: `services/model-api/analysis/phase*/`, `artifacts/phase*/`, `PREREGISTRATION_L*` drafts and
     `docs/recovery/`.
   - Both edit this file, but only their own task rows plus appended Log lines.
3. **Git is LOCAL only.** Commit and push only after the OWNER says yes. Before every commit, run
   `git diff --cached --name-only` and make sure no `*.db*`, `*.bak`, per-user models or datasets are staged.
   Commit messages never mention Anthropic, Claude or AI tooling.
4. **Reviews don't block.** CLOUD reviews LOCAL's code tasks in parallel. If CLOUD logs a problem, it is fixed
   before the commit.
5. Nothing confirmatory runs before its pre-registration is approved by the OWNER and hashed.

  1. **Which client runs the study?** PROTOCOL.md §2 has participants on their own Chrome with "Continuum running in the background" during ordinary work, i.e. the **extension**. But the extension scores everything **on-device**: its keystroke model (`trainer.js`) trains against bundled impostors, not the Aalto negatives (T3.3), and its mouse model (`mouse-model.js`) is the unchanged on-device one, not S1. Its only server call is `/health`. So "decision rules as deployed: keystroke 10 of 20, mouse S1" is not what the extension runs, and nothing it scores reaches ca-service's logs. Options:
     - (A) **Recommended.** A study mode in the extension: it sends its derived windows (as the SDK already does) to ca-service, which scores them with the deployed server models (S1, Aalto negatives) and logs everything in one place (LOGGING_SPEC as written, one export).
     - (B) Study the on-device extension as it is: the logging and export move into the extension, and the protocol's rules become the extension's own (not S1, not Aalto).
     - (C) Web app + SDK only: server rules, but no "ordinary work in the background".
  2. **Privacy claim is inaccurate.** CONSENT.md says "Not recorded: the text you type … Typed text never leaves your browser", and PROTOCOL.md §4 says the live system sends "only derived timing windows". In fact:
     - the windows carry the exact key codes, `id = keyCode / 254` (`keystrokeSecurity.js` line 195);
     - the server path receives **raw** key events and stores them in `keystroke_events.key_code` in typing order;
     - an ordered key-code sequence is the typed text, with only case lost.

     Password fields are masked to 0 (extension `content.js` line 203). This matters for the ethics request. Options: (a) say it plainly in the consent ("the order of the keys you press is recorded, from which text could be reconstructed; it is stored pseudonymously and deleted after…"); (b) restrict study typing to the scripted tasks; (c) remove key identity from the features, which changes the model and needs a new evaluation.
  - Meanwhile, safe under A or C: mouse check scores are rounded to 4 decimals in `mouse_decisions` (the spec wants full precision), and the 440 LOSO scores are not stored per profile. I can fix both once A or C is chosen.

  - **Server, ca-service (behind `STUDY_LOGGING=1`; production behaviour unchanged when off).**
    - S1 `study_windows`: one row per scored window per check: participant, session id, channel, window key, client timestamp, full-precision score, condition, day since enrolment, check id. Window key: mouse = the client's per-session window counter; keystroke = the client timestamp of the window's last event, since windows are rebuilt from the event tail. The export dedupes on (session, channel, window key); overlapping checks re-score the same window with the same model, so identical scores are expected, and the export asserts it.

Mouse = the 440 LOSO scores: model-api computes them and `lib/mouse.js` keeps only percentiles today; keystroke = `validationGenuineScores`, which api.py already returns.
    - S3 `study_decisions`: check id, session, window range, low count, verdict, prompt shown, and outcome `passed` / `failed` / `dismissed` with timestamps (a new `/v1/study/prompt` route the step-up page calls).
    - S4 the study context `{participant, sessionId, condition, clientTs}` rides on the existing check bodies. The gateway already forwards the body; ca-service validates `condition` ∈ {`genuine`, `impostor:<pseudonym>`} and never infers it.
    - S5 `scripts/study_export.js`: one JSONL file per participant (profile records, windows, decisions); `study_replay.js`: the integrity check (re-derive every verdict from the exported scores and rule); a privacy grep test (no text, URL or page title).

    - E1: gateway URL, study site key, participant pseudonym, condition. Session id = one per page visit.
    - E2: gets a session from `/v1/sdk/session` (`externalId = study:<pseudonym>`; the study tenant allows the extension's origin); enrols on the server (5 keystroke samples, 8 mouse samples of 55); sends checks through `/v1/sdk/*` like the SDK. On-device scoring is off in study mode, so there is one system under test. Verdicts drive the same lock and step-up flow.
    - E3: password fields stay masked (key 0).
  - **Checks before the first participant:** the spec's §5 (scripted genuine and impostor sessions; replay reproduces live verdicts; privacy grep), plus all existing gates.

- [26 Sep 00:10] CLOUD, answering your 23:10 question: **yes, move live mouse from S1 to F2.** This is T3.4, decided under the OWNER's delegation. Your framing is right: the confirmatory claim is non-inferiority. The interval above 0 is descriptive, while the pooled-EER superiority is the pre-registered key secondary and it passed.

  Why adopt:
  - it is non-inferior on untouched data and better on pooled EER in both stages;
  - it is order-free (fixes D4);
  - it trains in seconds instead of 9 fine-tunes, and keeps no per-user network (TODO §5b, study enrolments).

  The rule stays as tested: p20 of leave-one-sample-out scores, 33 of 60. Disclose in RUN_LOG that on Bogazici, at that rule, F2 prompts more than S1 (median +0.43/min) while catching more (+19 pts). Ideally land it before the study starts, so the study runs F2.

  **Spec, to match stage C bit for bit:**
  1. **Cohort file, built once.** `services/model-api/build_f2_cohort.py` writes `artifacts/mouse-fcn-a5/f2_cohort.npz` with `mu` (128) and `C` = unit(Z − mu). Get both from `c_common.cohort_c(c_common.deployed_embedder(c_common.load_scaler_c()))`: 40 windows per base-100 user, seed 7. Call that function; don't re-implement it. Log the file's sha256 in RUN_LOG. Production then needs no SapiMouse data.
  2. **model-api training.** Add `train_user_f2_payload`, or `headScheme: "f2"` on the LOSO path:
     - E = unit(embed(scale(pos)) − mu), with embed = the deployed network's `embedding` layer, frozen and loaded once for all users.
     - neg = C[default_rng(21).choice(len(C), 2·len(E), replace=False)].
     - head = LogisticRegression(C=1.0, solver="lbfgs", max_iter=5000) on [E; neg], labels [1; 0].
     - LOSO: for each of the 8 samples of 55, fit on E[keep] + the same neg, where keep leaves out the held-out sample and 4 windows on each side (`l_common.loso_folds`), then score E[held].
     - Return `losoScores` and `losoScorePercentiles` exactly as `train_user_loso_payload` does, so `mouse.js` reads the p20 unchanged, and T4.3's S2 gets all 440 scores.
     - Store the head (coef, intercept) as JSON in the user's model dir, with the cohort file's sha and the base model's hash.
  3. **model-api prediction.** An F2 profile scores p = predict_proba of the stored head on unit(embed(scale(w)) − mu). No per-user TF model is loaded.
  4. **ca-service.** `mouse.js` sends the F2 scheme; `MOUSE_DECISION_VERSION` becomes e.g. `f2-loso-p20-33of60`, so S1 profiles are rebuilt from stored samples on the existing decision-version path. Then run `scripts/retrain_mouse_profiles.js` once.
  5. **Equivalence gate `validate_f2.py`.** Positives = 440 windows of 2 base-100 users (not test users). The production path must reproduce `l_common.head_scores` on the same inputs (LOSO scores plus 200 test windows) to 1e-9, and the cohort must equal `c_common.cohort_c` bit for bit.
  6. **Unchanged:** the extension's on-device head (not used in study mode) and the S1 code, which stays in the tree.
  7. **Gates.** Update e2e_mouse_a5.js and validate_pipeline's mouse checks. RUN_LOG cites `RESULTS_L_stageC.md`. I'll review the diff here.

- [28 Sep 19:15] CLOUD: **New task for LOCAL — secure the internal hops (OWNER asked 28 Sep 19:08: "X-Api-Key over http can be intercepted; why not RSA like gateway→ca?").** Current state, from the code: gateway→ca-service and ca-service→model-api are plain HTTP. ca→model carries only a static `X-Api-Key` (compose default `dev-model-api-key`); gateway→ca carries `X-Api-Key` plus an RS256 identity assertion (aud/iss/exp checked, `IDENTITY_MAX_AGE_SEC` 600, no jti, not bound to the request). Both can be sniffed by anything on the Docker network, and a captured assertion replays for up to 10 min. Proposed (implement in this order, gates after each):
  1. **mTLS on both internal hops** (the real fix: encryption + mutual authentication). A small internal CA generated at first start into a git-ignored volume (never committed); one cert per service (gateway, ca-service, model-api) with the service name as SAN; ca-service and model-api accept only TLS with a client cert signed by that CA; model-api additionally accepts only the ca-service cert (and trainer if needed), ca-service only the gateway cert. Node: `https.Agent({cert,key,ca})` / undici dispatcher for fetch; Flask: `ssl_context` with `ssl.CERT_REQUIRED`. Keep plain HTTP only behind an explicit dev flag that logs a SECURITY warning.
  2. **Replace the static ca→model key by a short-lived signed request token** (same pattern as the gateway assertion): ca-service signs `{iss: ca-service, aud: model-api, iat, exp ≤ 60 s, jti, sha256(body)}` with its own ES256/RS256 key; model-api verifies with the public key only, rejects reused `jti` (in-memory LRU for the token lifetime) and a body hash mismatch.
  3. **Tighten the gateway assertion:** add `jti` + request binding (method, path, body hash), lower max age to ≤ 60 s.
  4. Remove the `dev-*` default keys from docker-compose.yml for any non-dev profile (fail fast if unset, as the gateway already does for its secrets).
  Gates: validate_pipeline, e2e_mouse_a5, validate_endtoend, plus a negative test per hop (no cert → rejected; replayed token → rejected; tampered body → rejected). No model or data changes. RUN_LOG entry. CLOUD will update the defense deck (slides 5, 7, 20, 21, A15) once it lands.

- [9 Oct] LOCAL (branch `cloud-native`): **Step 5, ca-service on PostgreSQL (OWNER chose "I convert all": LOCAL may change only the database mechanics of the mouse session's files, no logic, proven by the decision replay and the mouse/study e2e, and logged here).**
  - `services/ca-service/db.js`: `STORAGE=sqlite` (default, the file, as before) or `postgres` (`DATABASE_URL`), behind one async interface (`db.get/all/run/insert/tx`). Every module uses it. On PostgreSQL the SQL is translated (placeholders, time functions, camelCase names) and values keep SQLite's form (timestamps as UTC text, JSON as text, 0/1 flags). Calls inside `db.tx` run in it (AsyncLocalStorage); a call from another request never does. The decision history's read-modify-write is row-locked. The training queue has leases (`locked_by`, `lease_until`, heartbeat) and one pending job per user and kind (unique index), so several copies can run. Express handlers are wrapped so a failed query is a 500, not a crashed process.
  - Sorts that could tie now break ties by `id`; checked on the real data that this is exactly the order SQLite returned (no result changes). The `ca` database uses `LC_COLLATE 'C'` (byte order, as SQLite). The identity seed tables (`principal_aliases` etc.) are in the schema because the tenant-wide reports read it (known gap, unchanged: it misses users who arrived after 2026-09).
  - `migrate.js`: one transaction, ids and id counters kept, unknown tables or columns refused, every table compared row by row. On the copy of the real data: 23 tables, 911,431 rows, identical (~20 s); the file was only read.

- [9 Oct] LOCAL (branch `cloud-native`): **Step 6, the model service: versions, object storage, two roles, gunicorn (OWNER: "Yes, storage only" for the mouse code, i.e. only where mouse models are saved and loaded; research scripts stay where they are, out of the production image).**
  - `services/model-api/model_store.py` (new): every training writes a new folder `users/user-<id>/<kind>-<UTC>-<hex>` and never changes it; ca-service passes the version with every scoring request; a version the keystroke validation gate rejects is discarded (`/api/user/rollback` with its path); the newest three per user and kind are kept, plus the current one. `MODEL_STORE=fs` (default) or `s3` (SeaweedFS locally): the trainer uploads each version and a marker with its fingerprint, a scoring copy downloads what it lacks and checks it. A feed of new versions lets every scoring copy load a new model before the user's next check (without it the first check after a retrain timed out on every copy: measured 5 no-verdict checks, then 0). Superseded versions leave a copy's memory after 10 idle minutes.

- [20 Oct] LOCAL: **Random-split sensitivity check done (`analysis/phase6/leaky_split.py`, 99 targets, 58.7 min, `artifacts/phase6/`, write-up `docs/recovery/RESULTS_LEAKY.md`).** The KS A200 recipe with the owner's windows split at random instead of in time: window EER mean 12.0% against 12.9% temporal (random minus temporal -0.9 points [-1.6, -0.2], lower for 61/99), window AUC 0.939 against 0.932. Reading: the personal models inflate only a little under a random split because their impostors never enter training; the big inflation (Buffalo base model 85.8% to 99.57%) needs both classes to leak, which is the case in Lu et al.'s protocol (negatives from the same people as the test impostors, random split with cross-validation). Two sentences added to paper 4-1 and thesis 4-1 saying so. Not pre-registered, reported as a sensitivity check. Nothing committed.

- [21 Oct] LOCAL: **Gap between evaluated blocks, 4 vs 5 windows (owner: "it should be 5, not 4").** A typing row is a digraph (keyCode a, keyCode b, dwell1, dwell2, dd, ud), so neighbouring rows share a key and a 50-row window spans 51 key presses. With `eval_decision_level.GAP = 4`, neighbouring blocks share no row but share one key press (the mouse: one pointer reading). With 5 they share nothing. Read-only check (scratchpad gap5_check.py, E.blocks patched, nothing written): typing A200 n=20 FAR/FRR/EER/AUC 11.9/3.3/7.82/0.9693 -> 12.1/3.6/7.77/0.9681; n=10 12.6/6.2/9.52/0.9582 -> 12.7/5.7/9.72/0.9605; n=6 12.4/10.1/10.99/0.9516 -> 12.8/9.4/11.16/0.9514. Mouse F2 FAR/FRR n=60 10.8/10.0 -> 11.1/10.0; n=33 12.9/10.0 -> 12.9/10.3; n=15 14.2/15.1 -> 14.4/15.5. Gap 4 reproduces the paper exactly. The train/test boundary (10 windows dropped) shares no key press at all. Recommended keeping 4: the paper claims only that no window is counted twice, the effect is tenths of a point, and eval_decision_level.py is hash-frozen in PREREGISTRATION_L. Awaiting the owner, nothing edited.
- [21 Oct] LOCAL: **Lu et al.** [1] attribution of the typing packet (open, found 2026-10-21). The paper (Table follow row "Typing: packet | Six windows [1]", 4-2 "decisions rest on a packet of windows, six in [1]. Under majority voting at 0.50", packet table row "6, as in [1]") and the thesis (part 2 l.129 "تصويت الأغلبية عند الحد 0.50 مأخوذة من [1]، حيث تتألف الحزمة من ست نوافذ", l.157, l.166 table, part 3 l.77 "كانت قاعدة [1] تتخذ القرار من ست نوافذ", l.83 "6 كما في [1]") attribute a six-window packet and majority voting to Lu. Lu's paper (PDF text, Figs 8-9 and Section 6 checked) has neither. The 0.50 cutoff per window is Lu's. Their packet is L keystrokes with a sliding window of C, giving L - C probabilities, and an intruder is flagged when more than p are below 0.50. In the live BBS test L = 50, C = 30, 20 probabilities, p = (L - C) x 15% = 3. After a legitimate packet the count restarts (Fig. 9 "Recount"), so live packets are consecutive and disjoint, as in our evaluation blocks. The 6 was our own old rule (3 of 6 until 23 Sep). Lu's rule on A200 (blocks of 20, gap 4, at least 4 low): FAR 5.0%, FRR 22.5%, against the adopted 11.9% / 3.3% (scratchpad lu_rule_check.py, read-only). Lu's reported results are single windows from 10-fold CV (80/20), with no time order and no gap. Fix proposed to the owner, nothing edited.

**4 Nov 2026 (gateway pages, this session):** the console (`/console.html`) and the operator page (`/operator.html`) switch to Arabic, right-to-left, with a language button on both sign-in screens and in the sidebar; the choice is kept per browser (localStorage) and the pages redraw without a reload. Design: the code stays English; `services/gateway/public/ui/i18n-ar.js` maps every English string to Arabic (keys = the English text; plurals as CLDR forms one/two/few/other); `ui.js` translates text children and placeholders inside `el()`, strings with placeholders through `C.t()`, counts through `C.plural()`, dates through `ar-SY` with Western digits; names, emails, keys and addresses go through `C.text()` and are never translated. CSS: sides written as inline-start/end so the layout mirrors by itself, the Arabic font (IBM Plex Sans Arabic from Google Fonts), no letter-spacing, arrows and the switch knob mirrored, codes/keys/addresses kept left-to-right; a left-to-right mark keeps `.env`, `https://`, `protect()` and `<script …>` intact inside Arabic. Tests: `services/gateway/test/i18n.test.js` (7, runs in CI) and `services/gateway/scripts/headless_ui_lang.mjs` (real headless Chrome against a simulated API, so no cluster is needed: 84 checks, every screen scanned for leftover English, modals, toasts, the switch back; screenshots). Rule for later edits to these pages: a new English string needs its Arabic in `i18n-ar.js`, or it shows in English (the harness flags it). **8 Nov 2026, architecture review (owner: "تاكد ما ينقص لمشروع ... لنبدا به"), read-only, nothing changed:** three parallel reviews (security/trust, decision pipeline/models, infra/ops) plus my own verification of the top items; report `../reviews/architecture_review_2026-11-08.html` (outside the repo). Verified: (P0-1) five `ca.db.pre-*.bak` copies (~45 MB each, one with 773k keystroke rows of 43 principals) and identity snapshots sit in services/ca-service and INSIDE the running ca-service image (`.dockerignore` excludes only `*.db`); the public Docker Hub ca-service tags of 30 Oct are 96 MB (clean CI builds) and the July tags predate the files, so no evidence they were published, but any manual `release.sh` from the working tree would publish them. (P0-2) the live edge listens on all interfaces; `http://<Wi-Fi IP>:8080/health` = 200 and the Docker Desktop Backend inbound rule allows the Public profile (his Wi-Fi is Public). (P0-3) newest full backup `_backups/continuum-k8s-backup-20261019-004321.tgz`, unencrypted, same disk as everything else; the daily job skips the demo DB and the models bucket. (P1-1) a typing check whose model call fails returns ok/"verified" and a signed "genuine" token (keystrokeSecurity.js catch returns isSuspicious:false with the full windowCount) — contradicts thesis 3-14 and ARCHITECTURE.md; mouse returns 503 correctly. (P1-3) continuum-node `requireTrusted` accepts any gateway-signed token unless risk === "suspicious" and treats a missing exp as never expiring; CAEP SETs carry no exp. (P1-4) degenerate input asks for the passkey but signs "genuine". (P2-1) the SDK mouse tracker uses page coordinates (documentElement rect), so scrolling becomes fake steps — a likely contributor to the owner's real-use mouse false alarms. (P3-1) `data/aalto_neg_A200.h5` and `data/mouse_only/Mouse_A5_W50_base.h5` (copied by Dockerfile.model.prod) are not in git. Other findings in the report: lock not consulted by check routes, SDK text gate on current text, paste via drop, permanent warm-up by >1 s gaps, key-repeat/229 tripping the degenerate rules, extension 503 and probation, reset during training, inactive retrain safeguard, no Postgres ADD COLUMN path, memory QoS, observability gaps, Grafana anonymous, SeaweedFS volume sizing; NetworkPolicies are not enforced on Docker Desktop (API keys are the real guard). Awaiting the owner's choice of what to start with.

**8 Nov 2026, P1 of the architecture review: fail closed on verdicts (owner: "خياران للمؤسسة", "الفشل مغلقًا", then "نعم، انشر الآن"). Commit e363cac, DEPLOYED 09:59:55 UTC.**
- ca-service: `evaluateKeystrokes` rethrows any scoring failure as ModelUnavailableError, so the route answers 503 `model_unavailable` with no token (it signed "genuine"); no typing profile → 200 `checked:false, rhythmEnrollmentRequired` with no token (unless paste/degenerate input asks for the passkey); `lockedAnswer()` makes every keystroke, mouse and mouse-action check of a locked account answer 423 `{locked, isSuspicious:true, requiresPasskeyChallenge:true}` signed suspicious; the answer and token carry `evaluation.isSuspicious || degenerateInput` (warm-up keeps the vote's own value for the SDK's probation). Only my two keystrokeSecurity.js hunks committed (patch on HEAD); the other session's comment hunks stay uncommitted.
- gateway: `hasPasskey(uid, { stepUp: true })` on the three check relays answers the last cached value, else "yes", when the passkey lookup fails (status/onboarding still read failure as "no"); `settleChannel()` keeps `<user>|keystroke` and `<user>|mouse` in gw_risk_state (new `storage.riskGet`), pushes the worse of the two on the user's key and signs it (riskChannels.js `combine`); a verified passkey (SDK and tenant routes) settles both channels genuine; the mouse relay passes a locked answer through signed suspicious; account deletion removes the channel keys too.
- @continuum/node and continuum-python: verdicts only (risk in genuine/warmup/suspicious, an exp, no event; else `not_a_verdict` / `no_expiry`); `requireVerified` / `require_verified` answer a warm-up 403 `verdict_pending` (no step-up); events have their own check (`verifyEvent` / `verify_event`) and the Node receiver applies each jti once, ignores events older than the last applied per subject or older than 24 h (202, so the outbox stops), and acknowledges test events without changing state. Acme's /save-note uses requireVerified (its page explains verdict_pending); apps/acme and apps/demo carry hand-made copies of the library (lib/continuumNode.js), both now equal to the package.
- Tests: ca-service 108/108 and gateway 60/60 with PostgreSQL (throwaway postgres:16-alpine; `scripts/ci-local.sh` itself failed today in `npm ci` for ca-service: no better-sqlite3 prebuild download and no Python for node-gyp in the CI container — environment, not code), new route tests (model 503, model 404, no profile, degenerate token, lock 423 on typing and mouse) fail on the old code and pass on the new; @continuum/node 3/3, continuum-python 3/3 (fixtures made with a throwaway key).

**8 Nov 2026, P2-1 (owner "اكيد", then "نعم، انشر" and "نعم، لهذا القياس"): the SDK mouse trackers no longer turn scrolling into movement. Commit df2a560, DEPLOYED 10:19:20 UTC (gateway and web-demo `:p21-df2a560`, built from a clean worktree, scanned, only the two tracker files differ).** `sdk/js/mouse-features.js` and `sdk/js/mouse-actions.js` record viewport coordinates (clientX/Y) and event timestamps, as the extension does; they recorded positions relative to the tracked element's box, and the live guard tracks document.documentElement, whose box moves with scrolling. Reproduced in headless Chrome with trusted CDP input: a 300 px wheel scroll with the pointer still, then a 3 px move, was one step of [3, 300] px; after the fix every step is at most 3 px. Regression test `extension/scripts/e2e_headless_sdk_scroll.mjs` (no stack needed) passes on the fix and fails on the old trackers. Both edges serve the fixed file at /js/mouse-features.js. Undo: retag `continuum/gateway:pre-p21-20261108` (51233cc8aaee) and `continuum/web-demo:pre-p21-20261108` (bb56c77c06bc). The "before" measurement on stored windows is impossible: `mouse_live_windows` is empty (windows are kept only for LIVE_CAPTURE_USERS, none set). Measure instead his mouse requests per minute of movement (suspicious, non-warm-up `mouse_decisions` x 1.6 s) after 10:19:20 UTC against the baseline since the current profile: 6 in 1,347 checks (~36 min of movement, 25 Oct to 5 Nov, injected minutes excluded) = ~0.17/min.

**8 Nov 2026, P2-2 to P2-7 of the architecture review (owner: "do all of steps without ask me and push anything you see correct"). Commit 04febc4, DEPLOYED.**
- Typing input. The SDK tracker and the extension's page script no longer record a held key's auto-repeat, IME key 229 or keys pressed during a composition; the extension records keys only in text inputs, text areas and rich-text editors. The text minimum counts the printable keys typed as well as the text left in the fields (SDK `typedLength`, ca-service `gateLength`). Text dropped into a field counts as a paste and is checked at once (SDK and extension); autofill, dictation and spelling suggestions (beforeinput insertReplacementText) are deliberately not counted. Typing that never fills a packet is unverifiable once twice the keys a packet needs were sent (`sparseTyping`: passkey, token suspicious). Not done from P2-5: a page reload still restarts the warm-up and the paste count (both live in the page's buffer; carrying them over needs server state, a design decision).
- Extension worker (P2-6). A 503 `model_unavailable` from one channel's check marks only that channel unavailable (its verdict dropped, popup row "not checked (model unavailable)", Arabic added); the other channel and collecting go on. Probation is kept in cg_progress. **The owner must reload the unpacked extension** to get content.js, background.js and the popup.
- Typing retraining (P2-7, ca-service lib/keystrokeSecurity.js and lib/trainingQueue.js). The gate read activity samples, which nothing has written since 2026-10-05, so it never ran; a retrain now holds its newest training sample out of the fit (one popup sample = 20 windows, used from the next retrain on) and both models score it; a new fit that cannot score it fails the training, a model in use that cannot score it leaves the new fit. A fit from scratch is a failed training. A reset no longer gets undone by a running training (samples checked before and after the save; the reset clears the samples first, then the profile, and cancels queued and running jobs with the new `trainingQueue.cancel`, so a new enrolment queues its own job). Only this session's hunks of keystrokeSecurity.js committed (HEAD + mine, staged with git update-index); the typing session's three wording hunks stay uncommitted. Thesis note for the owner: the deployed retrain now fits all samples but the newest, which it uses as the held-out check of the rollback the thesis describes (AR2 measured retraining on all samples).
- Tests: ca-service 120/120 on SQLite and PostgreSQL; new headless harnesses `e2e_headless_sdk_input.mjs` (6) and `e2e_headless_ext_input.mjs` (5), and steps 6-7 of `e2e_headless_ext_offline.mjs`; every new check fails on the previous code and passes on this one; the other stub harnesses (ext_paste, ext_use_sample, sdk_paste, sdk_popup, sdk_look, sdk_session_end, sdk_use_sample, sdk_mouse, sdk_scroll) all passed.
- Images from a clean worktree at 04febc4 (revision label), scanned clean, app-file diff = only the intended files. Deployed: ca-service `:p27-04febc4` (a6e097d5b313) 11:27 UTC; gateway `:p27-04febc4` (9ba466302173) and web-demo `:p27-04febc4` (9b7c8bf2527b) 11:38 UTC; both edges serve the new guard.js and keystroke-tracker.js. Live suites after the rollout: connect, enrol-reload, demo-backend all passed. Undo: `docker tag continuum/<svc>:pre-p27-20261108 continuum/<svc>:local` for ca-service (ad783d91977b), gateway (da1afb65371e), web-demo (d3a576017951), then `kubectl rollout restart` of that deployment.
- Incident (mine): a regression loop also ran `e2e_headless_study.mjs`, which runs against the LIVE stack (ca-service has STUDY_LOGGING=1), with a 240 s timeout that killed it before its cleanup. Its participant ZZE2E9XALJ (internal user 2185196276339160, created 11:30:41 UTC in the study-e2e organisation) was then cleaned with the harness's own steps (typing and mouse reset through the gateway, lock, passkey reset, study_windows/decisions/prompts/profiles/participants rows): profiles, samples and study rows are gone, as after a completed run; its identity row, two finished job rows and its decision rows remain, as after any run. Commit f2da7f3, DEPLOYED.** Found while ranking the review's open items: on the SDK routes the session alone let an impostor at an open session, even a locked one, register a passkey of his own (no check for an existing one, no lock check) and pass every step-up, or reset the typing and mouse profiles (ca-service's reset routes trust their caller to have verified a passkey; the gateway did not) and enrol his own behaviour; the demo's /api/webauthn/register/reset deleted the passkeys and cleared the lock on the session alone.

- SDK guard.js: resetProfile passes the token its verification returns. Demo: profile resets pass the session's stepUpToken; register/reset needs requireFreshPasskey; the dashboard and profile pages verify the current passkey before deleting; register/options passes Continuum's 403/503 through.

**9 Nov 2026, mutual TLS between the services (Istio ambient; owner: "install Istio and start stages 0 to 2", then "start" for 3 to 6).**
- Installed from `deploy/platform/istio` (1.31.4, pinned; rendered by a throwaway Helm container). In the mesh: `continuum`, `continuum-storage`, `continuum-demo`, `continuum-observability`, `envoy-gateway-system`, `cnpg-system`. Our four are STRICT with identity rules (`mesh-policies.yaml` in each base folder). No code or image changed; the service keys stay as a second layer.
- Docker Desktop needs five settings (`deploy/platform/istio/docker-desktop`, each with its reason): CNI and netns paths, native nftables, `/run` made shared by an init container, the agent waiting for every pod's IP, and 90-day certificates (ztunnel's renewal clock stops while the PC sleeps).

- `CONTINUUM_NETPOL_UNENFORCED` now defaults to off: the demo suite's "cannot reach the internal services" is a real check on Docker Desktop too.
- Measured, live, strict: connect, enrol_reload and demo_backend 132 passed, 0 failed, before and after a real Docker Desktop restart; the demo pod refused by ca-service, passkey-service, the model, PostgreSQL and the store. A daily-copy run through its own identity.
- The real restart left the CNPG operator outside the mesh (its IP reached the API after the agent's one scan). Re-added without a restart; the wait in the agent was added. A second real restart the same day (13:09 UTC) left every pod in the mesh and the three suites passed again (132, 0 failed); the wait ran and had nothing to wait for, so a late IP has not recurred since it was added.
- For any new service or path: add a rule to `mesh-policies.yaml`, or it is refused (`kubectl -n istio-system logs ds/ztunnel | grep "policy rejection"`). Mouse or keystroke work that adds a new internal call needs this too.

**12 Nov 2026, alerts (owner: "ok" to start; chose Windows notifications on his PC). DEPLOYED (he ran the apply).** Four Grafana rules in `base/observability/alerting/continuum-alerts.yaml`, evaluated every minute: service silent 5 min (no target_info), pod restarted more than twice in 30 min, any 5xx in 10 min, daily copy older than 26 h. Contact point = a webhook to `host.docker.internal:39871` = this PC, where `scripts/alert-notifier.ps1` (sign-in task "Continuum alerts", token CONTINUUM_ALERT_TOKEN in overlays/local/secrets/grafana.env, made by `k8s-local.sh secrets`) shows a Windows notification; nothing leaves the machine.
- Found while proving it: (1) the services' request counters cannot tell a restart from a second copy or a stopped copy (same labels; measured: 8 false "resets" on model-inference, 2 on ca-service, 0 real restarts), so a CronJob `pod-reporter` (python:3.11-slim, read-only RBAC on pods, every minute) publishes `continuum_pod_restarts`/`continuum_pod_ready` from the API, and the rule reads those; its identity had to be allowed at the collector (base/observability/mesh-policies.yaml). (2) `increase()` misses the first 5xx a route ever answered (a new series has nothing to grow from); the rule now adds a series that first appeared inside the window. (3) pg-backup's upload reports each success as a gauge over OTLP (overlays/local/telemetry.yaml sets the endpoint).
- Proven end to end: `extension/scripts/e2e_alert_fire.mjs` (a throwaway study-e2e participant with a profile whose model is missing: three real 503s; the rule fired in 52 s; the notification 31 s later). PowerShell 5.1 needs the .ps1 saved with a BOM (Arabic text).
- Limits he knows: notifications need the PC awake and "Do not disturb" off; Prometheus data is temporary, so rules need a few minutes after a Grafana restart.

- Owner's question answered the same day: the 3 h is the pause AFTER a retrain (adaptive_state.last_retrain_at), never a gap between samples; within a cycle samples are limited only by clean, non-overlapping typing. His account was never retrained. Design of 2026-10-04, measured; unchanged.

**13 Nov 2026, the alerts' first false alarm, fixed and DEPLOYED (apply went through).** "Server errors" fired at 11:40 local after the PC woke from sleep: no sample existed 10 min before the evaluation, so every old 5xx counter series the services re-sent looked new and counted whole (the 6 test errors of 12 Nov). The new-series clause is now multiplied by `clamp_max(count(up offset 10m), 1) or vector(0)` (the store was collecting ten minutes ago; covers sleep gaps and the pod's own restarts). Verified on the restart the apply caused: guard 0, old series "new" = 6, rule inactive for the first minutes. Also `startingDeadlineSeconds: 120` on the pod-reporter CronJob: the controller logged "TooManyMissedTimes" after the sleep, and past 100 missed runs it stops scheduling the job for good. The VM's /proc/uptime neither resets on a Docker Desktop restart nor runs during sleep: pod restartCount and the reporter's gauge are the truth.

**13 Nov 2026, live impostor replay on the owner's account after the scroll fix (owner: "جرب الان واختبر ان كان الحكم هشا"):** the owner feared the 0 alarms of 12-13 Nov meant the mouse verdict accepts anyone. Same protocol as 6-7 Nov: Claude in Chrome signed in to the demo as "ali" (his credentials, localhost only), dashboard.html loaded fresh (empty SDK buffer), SapiMouse user9's 1-minute session rescaled to his step size replayed as synthetic mousemove/mousedown/mouseup on document.documentElement with the recorded timing (viewport coordinates, mirrored at the edges so every step keeps its size; replay files from this session's scratchpad `ali_impostor/`, served from a temporary 127.0.0.1:8765 server, stopped afterwards). Result: the first check, at 09:31:59 UTC, 18 s after the start, found 48 of 60 windows low and locked the account (the 6 Nov offline prediction for this person: caught at the first check). His own profile is unchanged (threshold 0.2621, trained 24 Oct). The owner clears the lock with his own passkey. EXCLUDE from his genuine mouse statistics: 2026-11-13 09:31:30 to 09:33:00 UTC. His own movement in the demo on 12-13 Nov: about 14 min, 0 alarms (before the fix: 7 alarms in about 37 min); one-sided Poisson p ≈ 0.06, so "fewer alarms" is likely but not yet at the 5% line.

**14 Nov 2026, why the owner was asked for the passkey twice about ten minutes apart (owner: "استكشف السبب").** Read-only (ca and gateway tables, Tempo traces). (1) The first prompt was the lock left by the 13 Nov 09:31:59 impostor replay: the owner had not cleared it, so every check answered "locked" without being recorded (no mouse row from 13 Nov 09:32 to 14 Nov 13:55); he cleared it at 13:51:37 UTC (`/v1/sdk/passkey/auth/complete`). Lesson: after a live impostor test, confirm the lock is cleared before reporting done. (2) The second was a mouse decision at 13:55:51 UTC, the first full 60-window check after the buffers reset at the unlock: 34 of 60 windows below his threshold (the rule asks for passkey at 33). All 17 checks today came from the extension on ordinary websites (traces: 17 `/v1/sdk/mouse/check` from Chrome through Envoy, 0 `/v1/server/mouse/check`, the demo's route), and all sat at 27-34 low (median 29), against a median of 6 on 12 Nov and 0 on 13 Nov in the demo. The extension and the SDK build windows with the same code (clientX/Y, event timestamps, 0.5 s split, 50/10 windows, 0.08 path filter), and his profile is unchanged (threshold 0.2621, 24 Oct), so the shift is in the movement captured, not in the code: browsing ordinary sites, and possibly another pointing device or page zoom (the step-size confound of 25 Oct). Earlier days with high averages (3, 9 and 10 Nov: median 16-21) fit the same pattern. Not changed: the rule (measured) and his profile.
